Buy IMG Travel Insurance for a cruise or a long trip abroad in your 60s or 70s. Its iTravelInsured plans insure travelers up to age 99, with medical coverage that pays before your health plan and evacuation coverage up to $1 million. On a cheap domestic trip, check your credit card’s trip protections first.

VisitorsCoverage’s quotes for California residents, updated in August 2026, priced IMG’s Choice Cruise plan at $648 for a couple aged 72 and 70 on an $8,000, 10-day Bahamas cruise, about 8% of the trip’s cost. The Travel LX plan, which raises medical coverage from $100,000 to $500,000 and evacuation from $500,000 to $1 million a person, came to $1,089 for the same couple.

IMG fits cruisers in their 60s and 70s who want medical coverage that pays first and up to $1 million for evacuation, and travelers 65 and older spending up to a year abroad. A young traveler on a cheap domestic trip has little prepaid cost to lose and should check the trip cancellation coverage on the credit card used to book it before buying a policy.

Personal Finance (Not Investing) • Re: Is withdrawal rate modeling too simplistic to be useful to most people?

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Excuse me if I’m reading between the lines too much, but it seems like you’re trying to weigh the benefits of running out the clock by continuing to work vs. having a concrete plan that you can track your real retirement against. Most people I know definitely choose the former option, but I don’t think you need to overcomplicate the latter.

TPAW planner is a fairly good tool if you’re trying to figure out whether you can support temporary periods of higher spend. It gives you a month-by-month figure out what you could spend in the 5th, 50th, and 95th percentile of return outcomes. You could look at the range and see if it seems realistic to you. You will be projecting assumptions just like all the other tools, but I think it is clearer mental model to consider “could I live on $x” than it is to see it as a Y% of success.
Thanks for the TPAW rec! I've glanced it a few times but never concluded that it offered something more useful than I was getting from Pralana Online. To be totally transparent, I'm just iterating through possible scenarios in Pralana, but none of them rely on coming to any conclusion based on any year's withdrawal rate percentage. I mean, I do have numbers in the 4-5% range in mind when I look at what Pralana's deterministic projections show me needing to withdraw based on what I've told it about in expense assumptions but that's only casually useful as a sort of qualitative observation that most years are nicely below that 4-5% range. But I don't conclude anything from that. I much more heavily rely on Monte Carlo runs and testing sensitivity to things like my asset class return assumptions over time, AA, retirement date, LTC costs, how big a backstop the paid-off house would be, etc.

I think I was just wanting to validate that I'm not fundamentally missing some aspect of SWRs that people are leaning into. Your last sentence kind of sums up what's seemed off to me about SWR-based analyses:
“could I live on $x”
It's just too simple a question to try to answer in that form (unless you try to compensate like delamer suggested by modeling lifetime lumpy expenses as a fixed annual expense).

On OnlyFans, one-time transactions are winning out over subscriptions

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Back in August, OnlyFans announced that 5,076 creators have brought in at least $1 million each since the NSFW hub kicked off in 2016, with total creator payouts adding up to $30 billion. About 20% of that $30 billion—roughly $6.2 billion—was delivered to creators during the year that ended on November 30, 2025.

That last figure represents a solid increase in how much creators are making on OnlyFans—but the way they’re making money on the platform has evolved in recent years, too.

According to a recent report from Giggster, the vast majority of the $6.2 billion paid to OnlyFans creators in 2025 stemmed from non-subscription-based transactions. To compile its report (titled “Behind the OnlyFans Paycheck: Creator Payments, Platform Fees and U.S. Rankings”), the online marketplace combined “illustrative payment calculations with third-party estimates of OnlyFans creator accounts across all 50 states and Washington, D.C.” and examined “company-level revenue by payment type and revenue attributed to the U.S.”

Daily industry updates, straight to your inbox.

Overall, the report estimated that 700,000 OnlyFans creator accounts are active in the U.S., a number that makes up roughly 28% of the platform’s global total. And despite representing less than 30% of OnlyFans accounts, Giggster found that ~62% of OnlyFans’ platform revenue ($965.4 million) was attributed to the U.S. in the 2025 fiscal year.

One of the report’s most significant findings, however, relates to the rising importance of non-subscription-based revenue—aka payments from one-time transactions like tips, paywalled posts, paid direct messages, and custom content requests. According to Giggster, those payments generated 73% of OnlyFans’ platform revenue in the 2025 fiscal year (up from roughly 40% in 2020 and 60% in 2024).

OnlyFans isn’t the only creator-driven platform leaning into one-time payments, either. In a 2024 end-of-year post, Patreon noted that creators on its platform “increased monthly revenue of one-time purchases by 4x” year-over-year. More recently, a study from Ipsos commissioned by TikTok predicted that over 60,000 U.S.-based TikTok LIVE creators would make “more than the median part-time monthly income” from gift earnings in 2025.

YouTube has devoted significant resources to one-time transactions, too. In a January blog post, CEO Neal Mohan reassured creators that the video platform would “continue to invest” in “fan funding features like Jewels and gifts,” adding that “we know these are important revenue streams, especially outside the U.S., for creators like Santiago Matias, who uses Super Chat as a way for fans to influence the outcome of his hit reality series.”

At the end of the day, the rising importance of one-time transactions demonstrates an important truth for creators: if you want to earn more revenue, give fans more ways to pay you.

BBC pundit Chris Sutton has heaped praise on Florian Wirtz after the German playmaker delivered another impressive performance in Liverpool’s 3-0 Champions League victory over Club Brugge.

The 23-year-old continued his excellent recent run of form in Belgium, getting on the scoresheet as Andoni Iraola’s side secured a comfortable victory at the Jan Breydel Stadium.

Having endured a period of adjustment following his €125 million move from Bayer Leverkusen last summer, Wirtz is beginning to demonstrate why Liverpool invested so heavily in his services.

The German international has looked increasingly influential in recent weeks, with his growing confidence reflected in both his performances and attacking output.

Watching the game for BBC Radio 5 Live, former Premier League striker Chris Sutton singled out Wirtz for praise, highlighting the noticeable difference in his performances compared to last season.

Sutton said: “It’s a different Florian Wirtz this season, he’s just oozing with confidence.”

Operating centrally in Iraola’s 4-2-3-1 formation, Wirtz delivered another commanding 90-minute performance against Club Brugge.

The German dictated Liverpool’s attacking tempo, completing 41 passes and drawing three fouls as the Belgian side struggled to contain him.

With Liverpool leading 1-0 heading into the closing stages, Wirtz doubled the visitors’ advantage in the 79th minute with a stunning finish past Yann Sommer.

Alexander Isak added his second goal of the evening just three minutes later to put the result beyond doubt.

After recording eight goals and eight assists across 49 appearances during a steady adjustment period in 2025/26, Wirtz has hit peak form over the past month for both club and country.

His recent surge includes a dazzling run of end-product. He has now scored four goals and six assists this season across all competitions, including a goal and assist vs Manchester United on Sunday to help his side beat Mauricio Pochettino’s side 4-0.

Liverpool are finally seeing the world-class playmaker they broke the bank for last summer.

Despite December Reprint Window, MTG Zeta Set Cards Are Still Pretty Valuable

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

There's a lot to be said for the strong prices of Zeta Set singles when another Secret Lair window is less than two weeks away.

Despite many issues with its initial drop, Magic: The Gathering's Zeta Set product is finally in players' hands, and cards are starting to appear on the secondary market. While this isn't too surprising, the strong early values for some of the cards and the growth in recent days can be considered somewhat unusual, especially given that there is a print-to-demand window coming up on December 9.

While stock from this drop won't be in players' hands until at least mid-2027, a flood of cards could hit the market then, and those who don't want to wait will pay a premium to get cards sooner.

Some of the Zeta Set cards we're seeing at high prices early on are wholly expected. Take Chrome Mox, Birds of Paradise, and Urborg as good examples. However, in daily price tracking, we're seeing cards like Cauldron Familiar, Ward of Bones, Thought Vessel, Eerie Interlude, Go For The Throat, and Duress all up between 20% and 130%.

We had an inkling that, despite all the issues with players actually getting their hands on the product, The Zeta Set would go on to be a hit with players. Whether it's the unique artwork or the crucial rarity adjustments that change the legality of some cards in specific formats, it's clear that the product has an audience, even if the early backlash was quite seismic when the drop went pear-shaped.

Here's a breakdown of the cards that have grown the most recently from The Zeta Set:

Time will tell whether the prices of these cards are on the rise due to scarcity, or whether the set itself has some real legs and players are keen to start collecting these versions of cards. It's worth pointing out that listings are proving hard to come by, with most cards having a sales history on marketplaces like TCGplayer, but very few active listings, suggesting that the cards don't last long once they're actually available.

This controversial set comes in booster packs but is delivered via Secret Lair, leading to a lot of unrest and outrage from players.

The Zeta Set is heading back onto the Secret Lair website for a 24-hour print-to-demand window on Wednesday, December 9 at 9 am PT. This additional window makes up for the disastrous initial launch, when players were frozen in a queue for over 80 minutes after technical issues; then, when the sale restarted, stock vanished, and queue positions weren't maintained.

Zeta Set boosters have seven cards in each booster pack and cost $19.92 per pack.

Purchase limits still apply in this sale, with a maximum limit of 12 packs per account. There isn't a confirmed shipping date for packs ordered during this window; Wizards of the Coast has only stated they're expected to ship in the second half of 2027.

Tony Romo's first gig since CBS exit involves 'predictions' at Jake Paul-led company

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The former Dallas Cowboys quarterback and CBS broadcaster is joining gaming company Betr as its "Chief Predictions Officer." Betr was cofounded by Jake Paul.

After retiring following the 2016 season, Romo had been the NFL on CBS' lead game analyst. However, he and the network parted ways in October following his July arrest for operating a vehicle while intoxicated.

"Tony Romo has built an extraordinary reputation around something that sits at the very heart of our business: predictions," Betr CEO and cofounder Joey Levy said in a statement. "His ability to see what's coming before anyone else has made him one of the most compelling personalities in sports, and we see a tremendous opportunity to bring that talent and personality to our growing ecosystem."

According to The Hollywood Reporter, Romo's role will consist of him "appearing in original content, delivering sport-related prediction and serving as a face of the brand in marketing." He'll appear in videos for social media alongside Paul.

"I've spent my entire career trying to anticipate what's going to happen next, whether that was on the field or in the broadcast booth," Romo said in a statement. "Now I get to put those instincts to work in a completely new way with Betr. What Jake, Joey and the team are building is really interesting because they're bringing so many different ways for fans to engage with sports and gaming together in one place. I'm looking forward to having some fun, making predictions and being part of where this company is headed. And I've got a pretty good feeling about what's coming next."

Romo has been replaced in CBS' top NFL booth by JJ Watt, who now sits alongside Jim Nantz. Romo's first appearance in the public spotlight following his arrest, which included embarrassing body camera footage from the police, came during a "Monday Night Football" alternate broadcast stream with Pat McAfee.

2 Bargain Stocks to Buy With $1,000 in November

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

A drug maker and a food maker, both of which have attractive yields and strong businesses backing their dividends.

By Reuben Gregg Brewer – Nov 25, 2026 at 5:27PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Novo Nordisk ›

Novo Nordisk's (NVO +1.64%) yield is historically high at 4.4%. The same is true of PepsiCo's (PEP +0.88%) 4.1% yield. By comparison, the S&P 500 index's (^GSPC -0.25%) yield is a mere 1%. That suggests both are bargains as November draws to a close. Here's a closer look at why you should consider putting $1,000 into each one of these bargain stocks.

Novo Nordisk is a pharmaceutical company that is leaning into its Wegovy/Ozempic GLP-1 weight-loss portfolio. Wall Street is worried for two main reasons. First, it fumbled the ball after being the first to market with this new class of drugs. Second, it is shifting its business model to one driven by volume, which has required it to lower prices.

As to the first concern, Novo Nordisk's execution of its GLP-1 shot wasn't great, as it struggled to keep up with demand. It has been much better with its recent pill launch, which has seen dramatically higher demand. Moreover, there's likely room for more than one weight-loss drug given the size of the addressable market, so falling into second place in the GLP-1 space isnt the end of the world. Meanwhile, lowering prices has, indeed, hurt revenues and earnings. But the company upped its full-year 2026 guidance when it reported third-quarter 2026 earnings. That suggests that the volume-led transition is going well.

Novo Nordisk's 4.4% yield is historically high and well above the pharmaceutical industry average of 1.3%. Its roughly 10x price-to-earnings ratio is also well below the industry average of 27x. It looks like a bargain for more aggressive income investors, and a $1,000 investment will net you roughly 24 shares.

PepsiCo is one of the world's largest consumer staples companies. It is an industry leader in the snack and beverage areas, and it has a sizable packaged food business as well. It is more diversified than most of its peers, and it can stand toe-to-toe with any of them as a business, with strong brands, marketing, innovation, and distribution.

The problem is that PepsiCo's business isn't firing on all cylinders right now, with its organic sales growth notably lagging that of key peer Coca-Cola (KO -0.03%). Still, PepsiCo's 3.1% organic sales growth in the third quarter wasn't bad, even though it was less than half of Coca-Cola's 7%. But investors remain worried, thanks notably to weakness in PepsiCo's large U.S. operations.

That's fair, but PepsiCo is a Dividend King, with over 50 annual dividend increases. It has been through difficult times before and survived. And it is making the types of decisions you would expect during the current difficult period, including acquiring desirable brands and introducing innovative new products. It is highly likely that the company will get through this and thrive again over the long term.

If you buy it now, however, you get to collect the historically high 4.1% yield. And you are buying it while its 18x P/E is notably below its 25x five-year average. For reference, the average consumer staples stock yields 2% and has a P/E of around 26x. A $1k investment will buy roughly seven PepsiCo shares.

To be fair, neither Novo Nordisk nor PepsiCo is about to suddenly break out of their funk. So you have to go in with a long-term mindset. However, you'll get to collect a well above-market income stream while you wait for these historically well-run businesses to get back on track. And given their recent performance and historical results, it seems highly likely that they will eventually do just that.

A Very Serious Story About Falling Asleep While Belaying

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The East Face of Mount Temple, Canada, and the scene of this story's shenanigans. (Photo: Mitra Samavaki / Getty)

Big falls are commonplace while sport climbing and bouldering, but, in my opinion, the high alpine remains largely a safe space to take your brake hand off the rope. Why? With loose rock, aerated ice, and an often minimalist rack, falling off—even with a tight belay—usually spells disaster. A climber has every reason to follow the old “the leader cannot fall” ethos.

This old school state of mind deeply resonated with me earlier this month on a difficult alpine climb on Mount Temple in the Canadian Rockies. This 1,400-meter ice and mixed route began easily enough, with a hike up a hard-packed snow slope at 6 a.m. toward the deep cleft that marked the start. Neither my partner Rory nor I were burdened with rope management during the first hour of climbing, since our rope stayed neatly coiled in a pack.

Hundreds of meters higher, below one of the crux pitches, we flaked out our rope. Above us stood a terribly skinny pillar of ice no wider than my shoulders at its base. Blue light filtered through its entirety, a sign of its youth and untrustworthiness.

“I don’t think I should place a screw in that,” Rory decided.

I scanned the shattered quartzite behind the pillar, hoping to at least keep us attached to the mountain before Rory essentially soloed the pitch. Two nervous pitons and a shallow micro nut were all I could cobble together after 10 minutes of searching. I stomped out a waist-deep hole in the snow on the back side of the pillar and clipped into our wobbly anchor. My now four-piece anchor—plus Rory’s unshakeable climbing ability—gave us great odds of surviving his lead.

The runout pitch gave me ample opportunity to pay out slack as I pleased while munching on trail mix and rehydrating from our busy morning. After all, Rory was not allowed to fall, so he didn’t.

Falling has been a frowned-upon practice for most of climbing’s history, especially before sport climbing became popular in the 1980s. Only relatively recently, and only in some styles of climbing, have leader falls become no longer taboo—even acceptable and encouraged.

The crusty old-timer in me knows that, in any form of free climbing, it is always safer to climb to the top of a pitch without testing your equipment, be it a skinny rope, crashpad, or ice screw. This gut instinct evolved from a time when climbing gear was not completely trustworthy. Maybe you hand-stitched your quickdraw’s webbing yourself. Maybe you’d sourced your climbing protection from a hardware store. Or maybe you had tied your lead line around your waist because drop-seat harnesses hadn’t been invented yet. In this era—really only a few decades ago—your climbing ability was the first line of defense. Ropes and harnesses and removable protection served merely as dubious back-ups.

But the advent of regular whippers is a tragedy for one more reason: With newfangled belay devices, hang dogging, and constant pleas to “watch me!” belaying has become a real pain in the ass.

Nearly 12 hours later, Rory and I were still on Mount Temple. We’d topped out the ice climbing portion of the face and traversed across open snow slopes and through rock bands to a final serrated crux: the Black Towers. Countless turrets of dark limestone slice across the summit ridge of Mount Temple, creating difficult route finding even on a clear summer’s day. We were approaching midnight in November and a thick fog had choked the upper mountain. Visibility hovered around 10 meters. I had no idea where we were supposed to go.

Thankfully it was Rory’s lead again, and he was questing. Fifteen minutes ticked by as he wandered up a steep rocky groove, then another 15 of the rope unmoving. An hour passed. I clicked off my headlamp to save battery. More time passed. I figured I could tie off the rope and catch a few Zs, so I did. Sleep felt amazing. I was glad that even as we approached 24 hours on the move, Rory still knew the rules: The leader cannot fall. And the belayer gets to sleep.

I woke up to the sound of Rory’s voice. He’d been blocked by an overhang and was downclimbing. Thick, icy fog obscured our vision as I took the rack and traversed along a snow ledge looking for easier passage. The fog would sometimes lift for an instant—I’d spot a lonely star above—and I’d frantically flick my headlamp to full strength to catch a glimpse of the mountain. But before my eyes could adjust to the new sight, the fog would crash back in like a breaking wave, drowning us in uncertainty once again.

This story does not end in a climbing success. We did not ultimately find our way through the Black Towers, nor did we share a weary, satisfied hug on the summit of Mount Temple. We became lost and a little delirious on a large mountain in the middle of the night and needed to go down.

We stayed roped together as we retraced our steps through deep snow on the upper mountain. I was a little worried about triggering an avalanche, but mainly I was just exhausted. If I stumbled, I would prefer to slide 30 meters before being caught by the rope than well over a thousand into the next life.

Rory and I regrouped at an anchor I’d built in an outcrop of yellow rock. He led out across another snowfield which terminated at a gigantic notch in an otherwise sheer ridgeline. The notch marked the start of a classic ski descent, the 60-degree Aemmer Couloir, and our escape hatch. Rory trudged toward it and I paid out rope, nodding off again.

Suddenly, I was awake. I had not pooped all day, yet the kilogram of trail mix and sausage and cheese I’d downed was making a basement appearance.

“Rory! Do you need a real belay for this bit? I really need to poo,” I yelled into the fog.

I let the rope feed through the anchor, then unclipped it from my belay device and, without moving even a step away from our anchor, unloaded into the snow bank.

It would have been a disgusting mess had Rory fallen and weighted the anchor, dragging me into my own steaming waste. But he didn’t, and I made it back to civilization with both my dignity and my life.

Austin Reaves talks for first time ahead of Spurs clash: ‘Felt like I broke my foot’

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Austin Reaves, who has missed the last five games because of a bruised left heel, was a full participant in Lakers practice on Wednesday but his status remains unknown for Friday's game against the Spurs.

"It felt good to play basketball again," Reaves said in his first public comments since suffering the injury Nov. 11. "Wind was a little bad, but kind of to be expected being out two weeks. But felt good. You know, we'll see how I wake up [Thursday]. We're still going in the right direction, but felt really good today."

Austin Reaves, left, and Luka Doncic talk during a game against the Utah Jazz in Los Angeles, Wednesday, Nov. 18, 2026. NBAE via Getty Images

Reaves sustained the injury after landing awkwardly following a missed fast-break dunk in the third quarter of a game against the Bucks.

He said he's not quite sure what happened on the play.

"No, I had no idea," he said. "Tried to play through it, and then I drove baseline, planted on it, and was like, I can't do this. So walked to the back and was in some pretty bad pain. I didn't think I broke my foot, but it sure felt like I broke my foot."

Lakers coach JJ Redick, who suffered a similar injury in April 2016, has repeatedly described the heel bruise as a "pain tolerance issue." Reaves echoed that Wednesday.

"It's not a strain in your hamstring or something like that," Reaves said. "It's kind of just something that's going to linger around if you continue to play before you're ready. And basically, the main message was, let's get this back to normal before we put you back out there and put yourself at risk again so later on in the season you won't have to deal with a flare up or something."

The Lakers went 4-1 after Reaves suffered the injury, but they clearly missed his offensive production after falling to the Jazz on Nov. 18, 109-97, and barely squeaking past the Hornets (118-114) and Kings (106-104). Reaves is averaging 24.7 points (51.3% shooting from the field, 40.9% from beyond the arc), 3.3 rebounds and 4.7 assists.

"One of those things that just, it sucks," Reaves said. "You know, I was rolling, playing well. We were rolling as a team, wanted to continue to be out there and play games. Hate missing games. But it's one of those things, like I said, there wasn't much that we could really do. But we took care of business."

Austin Reaves #15 of the Los Angeles Lakers reacts during a game against the Utah Jazz at Crypto.com Arena on Monday, Nov. 23, 2026, in Los Angeles. NBAE via Getty Images

When asked how Reaves looked Wednesday, Redick didn't hesitate.

When reporters prodded him to see if he was being sarcastic, he doubled down, "Yeah, not joking," he added.

Austin Reaves (15) of the Los Angeles Lakers smiles during a game against the Utah Jazz at Crypto.com Arena in Los Angeles on Wednesday, Nov. 18, 2026. NBAE via Getty Images

The Lakers sure hope that Reaves can return Friday and that Redick is right considering the challenge they have ahead of them.

The Lakers and Spurs are tied for the best record in the NBA at 15-3 and Luka Doncic and Victor Wembanyama are currently in a two-man race for the MVP Award.

When asked if it's important to him to return against the Spurs, Reaves balked.

"I mean, I want to play," Reaves said. "…I didn't get hurt the night of Milwaukee and look at the schedule and be like, 'God, I need to be back before the San Antonio game.' If I'm back, good, we'll go to war. But I got to listen to my body and take care of myself. But it should be fun if I'm healthy and get out there."

Aaron Rodgers Released by Steelers After ‘Pat McAfee Show’ Appearance

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The Pittsburgh Steelers cut the four-time NFL Most Valuable Player on Wednesday, after comments he made about Steelers coach Mike McCarthy during an appearance on the ESPN’s show.

Aaron Rodgers came to Pittsburgh in a Chevy.

The Pittsburgh Steelers cut the four-time NFL Most Valuable Player on Wednesday, shortly after he torched Steelers coach Mike McCarthy during an appearance on ESPN’s The Pat McAfee Show.

The move capped a chaotic 18 hours in which the team announced it was benching the 42-year-old future Hall of Famer for backup Mason Rudolph ahead of Friday’s visit from Denver. Related Stories Business Tony Romo Lands Post-CBS Gig With Betting App Betr as "Chief Predictions Officer" (Exclusive) TV Tom Jackson, Denver Broncos' All-Pro Linebacker and ESPN Analyst, Dies at 75

Rodgers responded by taking an excused absence from the club, then used the unexpected time off to go on McAfee and repeatedly call the 62-year-old McCarthy’s mental state into question.

Rodgers told McAfee he found out about the surprise decision while on the phone with teammates, including tight end Pat Freiermuth.

It was just the start of a blistering offensive from Rodgers, who painted McCarthy — whom he played under during a long successful run in Green Bay earlier in their careers — as a leader woefully out of touch.

He described a quarterback meeting last Wednesday with McCarthy arriving late, sat down “and within 10 minutes began snoring,” woke up and nodded off again minutes later, then eventually “walks out of the room like nothing happened.” Rodgers also said McCarthy missed an offensive meeting Thursday, missed a walkthrough Friday and a morning meeting Saturday while not holding a team meeting that night.

“And then comes to Sunday, when obviously we struggled and the product wasn’t great,” Rodgers said, adding that McCarthy broke up the halftime locker room meeting by saying “1-2-3 Packers.”

Rodgers said he felt it was the right thing to bring up the issues to McCarthy as a longtime league star instead of putting it on others with fewer years, shaky roster positions or those who “haven’t made $400 million dollars in the NFL.”

“Me? I don’t care, what are you going to do to me?” Rodgers said. “Like I said, you’re going to release me? Cool. You’re going to deflect this thing and make it that you were trying to bench me and that I’m at home contemplating whether or not I’m going to accept a benching? Couldn’t be farther from the truth.

“That word never came up in our meetings. What came up was: sleeping, habits, gameplan, understanding what we’re doing, being able to call a play, being able to call a game without the game sheet.”

The scene played out bizarrely during Rodgers’ extended appearance on the show in which he has been an occasional contributor. McCarthy waded through a throng of assembled reporters in the lobby — most of them huddled over their phones listening to Rodgers — after practice ended.

Minutes later in a subdued locker room, the Steelers left to pick up the pieces declined to get into specifics about Rodgers’ accusations but didn’t dismiss them either.

Asked if he’s confident McCarthy can lead the team going forward, Freiermuth responded with a simple “I’m confident” but little else.

Wide receiver Marquez Valdes-Scantling, who played alongside Rodgers and under McCarthy as a rookie in Green Bay in 2018, acknowledged there was general frustration about the team’s recent performance but allowed “I didn’t think it was this.”

Rodgers has already said this will be his last season. Now it’s fair to wonder if he’ll reach the end of it — in Pittsburgh anyway — after McCarthy turned to Rudolph, a longtime backup, with the Steelers (6-4) trying to snap out of a two-game funk before it’s too late.

There’s little precedent for sitting a future Hall of Famer and Super Bowl winner midseason. Johnny Unitas, playing out his final days in a Chargers uniform, got benched for rookie Dan Fouts after four games in 1973.

Joe Namath never played again after throwing four picks in a loss to Chicago during a late-career cameo for the Los Angeles Rams a month into the 1977 season.

More recently, Denver replaced Peyton Manning with Brock Osweiler midway through 2015 even with the club sitting at 7-2. Manning returned in the season finale and then served as a game manager in the playoffs as the Broncos’ dominant defense led the franchise to its third Super Bowl victory.

Manning walked off the field with the Lombardi Trophy in tow in his final game.

While the Steelers are currently in playoff position, their next three games are against AFC West-leading Denver, surging Houston, and AFC South leader Jacksonville. While they can probably withstand one loss or maybe even two, three could be catastrophic.

Enter Rudolph, who rescued Pittsburgh’s season and briefly earned folk hero status in 2023 by coming off the bench in December and authoring a surge to a playoff berth.

That didn’t stop the Steelers from moving on from him immediately, bringing in Russell Wilson and Justin Fields in 2024 and then Rodgers in 2025, a move designed to help Pittsburgh end a postseason victory drought that’s nearing a decade.

Rodgers’ first year ended with a blowout playoff loss at home to the Texans. His future seemed uncertain until the Steelers hired McCarthy to replace Mike Tomlin in January, a job Rodgers played a hand in helping McCarthy land, though how big a hand is up for debate.

Not that it mattered in the end. Rodgers opted to make one last run at a championship while reuniting with the coach he won a Super Bowl with in early 2011.

A storybook ending on the field seems remote. Pittsburgh is a woeful 2-4 on the road, where it figures to be when the playoffs start if the Steelers can’t track down Cincinnati in the race for the AFC North title.

And the team’s Jekyll/Hyde play in general has offered little tangible proof that it is a legitimate threat to have an extended postseason stay.

Rodgers’ play has mirrored that of his team. He tossed three touchdowns in three consecutive weeks, even outdueling Cincinnati star Joe Burrow in a victory over the Bengals in late September.

Yet he’s struggled to make an impact of late, often checking the ball down to running backs Jaylen Warren and Rico Dowdle or taking sacks behind an inconsistent offensive line when trying to push the ball down the field to a receiving group that has underperformed, particularly two-time Pro Bowler DK Metcalf.

Rodgers averaged just 0.5 air yards per attempt in the first half last week against Philadelphia, the lowest since ESPN began measuring the stat in 2006.

What happens next to a player who has always leaned into doing things on his terms at all times is unclear. Pittsburgh was supposed to be his third and final NFL stop.

He seemed content on Tuesday, spending time in the locker room trading jokes with Rudolph and longtime defensive tackle Cam Heyward after addressing reporters.

Barely eight hours later, Rodgers was no longer the starter. Shortly after that, he was no longer a Steeler, the latest and perhaps final twist in a journey that will end with him getting a gold jacket at the Pro Football Hall of Fame, even if this ends up being an inglorious off-ramp for all involved.

AP Pro Football Writer Rob Maaddi and AP Sports Writer Aaron Beard contributed to this story.

US Police Share Statement as English Footballer Shot and Killed in Mississippi

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Tragic reports have emerged from the United States, confirming that a young English footballer was shot and killed in Mississippi. The local police have now shared a statement after making an arrest.

Krissh Mahatma was born in London but had moved to the US to pursue his dream of making it as a footballer. The 18-year-old striker was in his freshman year at Copiah-Lincoln Community College in Wesson, around 45 miles south of Jackson, Mississippi, where the incident took place.

Before moving to the US, he had played for Watford 'A' in the post-16 Community and Education Football Alliance league. Co-Lin announced his signing in February.

As reported by the Daily Mail, Mahatma was fatally struck by a bullet after a gun was fired outside the Green Ghost Tacos restaurant in Jackson at around 3:39 a.m. on Sunday morning.

The English footballer was with his Co-Lin Wolves teammates when they reportedly came across a drunk man outside the restaurant. After a fight broke out, a man pulled out a gun and fired into the group, hitting Mahatma.

The 18-year-old was taken to a nearby hospital via a private vehicle. According to Hinds County Coroner Jeramiah Howard, this was where he died.

Capitol Police have since arrested and charged Danny Luna, 22, with murder. The Mississippi Department of Public Safety did not elaborate on what caused the deadly confrontation. However, in a brief statement, Police Chief Mark Miller sent 'thoughts and prayers' to Mahatma's 'family and loved ones,' adding:

"While this arrest is an important step toward justice, we recognise that nothing can ease the pain of losing a loved one."

Is the Premier League really the best in the world?

Mahatma had made 14 appearances in his first three months with the Co-Lin Wolves, scoring one goal in September in a 3-2 win over National Park College. Co-Lin president Dewayne Middleton issued his own statement on behalf of the college:

"Copiah-Lincoln Community College is mourning the loss of freshman soccer player Krissh Mahatma. Krissh will be deeply missed by his teammates and the entire Co-Lin family."

A fundraiser has been set up to support Mahatma's family and has already raised $31,851 (£24,275). It explained how the young striker was "just beginning a new chapter of life far away from home," before adding: "His sudden passing has left his family, friends, teammates, coaches, and the entire Co-Lin soccer community heartbroken.

"Behind this tragedy is a family grieving the loss of their son and loved ones trying to make sense of a loss that feels impossible to put into words."

The owner of the Green Ghost Tacos eatery, nearby where the shooting took place, said: "On behalf of the entire Green Ghost family, I extend my deepest and most heartfelt condolences to the family, friends, loved ones, and university community mourning the tragic loss of a young student. Our hearts are heavy, and our thoughts and prayers are with everyone affected by this devastating incident.

"While this tragedy occurred near our establishment, we would like to clarify that Green Ghost was closed and was not conducting business at the time of the shooting. The safety and well-being of our patrons, employees, and surrounding community remain a top priority. We are committed to cooperating fully with law enforcement as they investigate the circumstances surrounding this tragic incident.

"Out of respect for the grieving family and the investigation, we will refrain from speculating or commenting further on the details at this time. Above all, we recognise that a family has suffered an unimaginable loss. We ask that the community join us in extending compassion, prayers, and support to those affected during this incredibly difficult time."

12 Pieces of Home Gym Equipment Under $100 – Buy Only What You’ll Use

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

Build a home gym from two or three pieces that fit the workouts you’ll actually do, not a full set. Resistance bands, a kettlebell, and a jump rope cover strength and cardio for about $75 new. Buy the kettlebell and other weights used, since iron doesn’t wear out.

A corner of a room holds a working home gym, and each of the 12 pieces below costs less than $100 new.

Body-weight moves like squats, lunges, and pushups need no equipment, so buy a piece only when it adds something your body weight can’t, such as something to pull against, extra weight, or cardio that fits a small room.

Match the pieces to the workouts you already do, or to the ones in your home workout plan. Bands and a kettlebell start a strength routine, a jump rope or a step covers cardio, and a mat and a foam roller cover yoga, Pilates, and stretching.

Say a beginner pays $45 a month for a gym she rarely uses. Bands, a 20-pound kettlebell, and a basic mat cost about $79 new, less than the $90 she pays for two months of dues. Canceling saves her $540 a year, so she’s $461 ahead after the first year, and all she gives up is machines and heavy weights she rarely used.

Kettlebells and dumbbells are the pieces to buy used, since cast iron weighs the same after decades of use. Look for them on Facebook Marketplace, on Craigslist, and at used sporting goods stores such as Play It Again Sports, which buys and sells used fitness gear.

The Play It Again Sports in Novi, Michigan, for one, charges 69 cents a pound for used dumbbells and 99 cents for new hex dumbbells, so a used pair of 10-pounders costs $13.80 there, $6 less than new.

Buy bands new, since a set costs as little as $13 and rubber wears out. Performance Health, which sells TheraBand bands, says to check bands for tears, cracks, and other wear before each use and to replace any that show it.

Most of the kit fits in a closet corner. Bands, a jump rope, sliders, and a suspension trainer share one bin, the mat and roller stand on end, and the weights sit on the floor beside them. A step with its removable feet taken off slides under a bed, which leaves the stability ball as the one piece that needs its own spot.

Buy the two pieces your favorite workout needs, use them for a month, and add a third only when a workout you’ve kept up calls for it.

Will Ferrell’s Netflix Golf Comedy ‘The Hawk’ Canceled After One Season

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Will Ferrell is not going to be thankful for this Wednesday-before-Thanksgiving bad-news dump. Ferrell’s out-of-bounds golf comedy The Hawk has been canceled by Netflix after just one season, The Hollywood Reporter has learned.

Ferrell and Netflix are not done with each other, however: Ferrell is executive producing new Netflix series All the Rage, Prep, and Nikki Glaser’s first (unnamed) scripted series. (Part of her Netflix Is a Joke Studios deal.) Ferrell produces for his Gloria Sanchez banner.

The problem with The Hawk was, well, it was (very) subpar. The Hawk, co-created by Ferrell and Harper Steele, followed disgraced pro golfer Lonnie Hawkins (Ferrell) who seeks to regain his former glory. Molly Shannon played Ferrell’s wife and Jimmy Tatro placed their son Lance. Related Stories Movies Scott Caan Thought He Got a Prank Text. It Was Actually David Fincher Wanting Him in 'Cliff Booth' TV 'The Body' Star Kristina Bogic Just Wanted to Take Care of Anya: "A Lot of Young Women Can Relate"

In his review, THR TV critic Daniel Fienberg called The Hawk “disposable,” writing that, “In general, The Hawk is a show that consistently operates at a barely minimal level, starting with the credits song, a slice of ’80s anthemic rock that contains the lyrics, ‘They call him the Hawk/ He’s a golfer/ The greatest golfer in the world.’ Wait. Sorry. The song doesn’t contain those lyrics — those are the only lyrics. It’s a piece of cheeky minimalism and a promise delivered.”

“There will be countless worse shows this year, but it’s doubtful that 2026 will offer a show as hollow and forgettable as The Hawk,” Fienberg scathed.

In addition to Ferrell and Steele, Jessica Elbaum, Alix Taylor, Rian Johnson, Ram Bergman, Nena Rodrigue, Chris Henchy, David Gordon Green and Andrew Guest also executive produced The Hawk.

The news comes as no surprise to the series’ creators. Earlier this month, Steele appeared on Late Night With Seth Meyers, calling The Hawk “short-lived” but also “probably the best show ever.”

The Hawk premiered on Netflix on July 16.

Deadline first reported the news of The Hawk‘s cancellation.

Arsenal Thought They Signed a 'Phenom' in Miyaichi, but things went wrong

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Back in 2011, Arsenal had built up a reputation for identifying some of the most talented players, whether it was Nicolas Anelka, Aaron Ramsey or Theo Walcott. The Gunners did some wise business, and they thought they had done the same with Ryo Miyaichi when they plucked from obscurity in 2011, offering him a trial.

Arséne Wenger was impressed by Miyachi when he had a trial with the club, and he offered the Japanese youngster a professional contract at the end of January of 2011.

The Frenchman said at the time: "We’re delighted that Ryō Miyaichi has joined us. He trialed with us in the summer and has raw ability which has attracted many clubs around the world". Some believed at the time that he could be the 'next Lionel Messi'.

"Ryo has natural technical ability. He has good balance and phenomenal pace, and his passing and crossing is consistently of a high quality. He is a very exciting player." - Wenger on Miyaichi in 2011

Miyaichi was an ultra-skillful player who quickly impressed in the youth ranks, both for club and country, and he was on course to become a star. In fact, during his first senior spell in professional football, on loan at Feyenoord, he was nicknamed 'Ryodinho' by the Dutch media, which only sparked excitement among Arsenal fans.

"I like his pace, his mobility and his movement off the ball. I like players who can move when others have the ball. There is also his attitude, commitment and desire. That is why I decided to keep him." - Wenger on Miyaichi in 2011

He went on to spend time at Bolton Wanderers and then Wigan Athletic, where he suffered a long-term ankle injury that saw him miss their successful FA Cup campaign.

By this point, Miyaichi had managed just one senior league appearance for Arsenal, and after a loan spell with Twente back in the Netherlands, he was released by the Gunners. Miyaichi later reflected: "Back then, I didn't really think of myself as young. There were so many world-class players around my age, and I was always feeling pressed."

The Japan international found a new permanent home at St Pauli, but his debut was significantly delayed by an ACL rupture, and after returning to action, he ruptured the ACL in his other leg in a horrible run of luck.

Remarkably, the German side still renewed the winger's contract. Sporting chief Andreas Rettig explained: “Ryo has unbelievable bad luck, and we do not want to leave him alone. [We want to] show that we are behind him. We hope that this decision will help Ryo to return to his old level of performance.”

Reflecting on his career at a later date, Miyaichi went on to say: "At one point, I seriously thought about quitting soccer.

"I have been away from the team for long periods and have caused trouble time and again. I have also disappointed many people. That is why I thought about quitting."

He went on to enjoy a couple of seasons of full fitness before being released in 2021 after racking up 77 league appearances. It was at that stage that he returned to Japan, joining Yokohoma F. Marinos, where he has been since, racking up 104 league appearances and scoring eight times. Back in 2022, he actually added three more Japan caps to his name, which must have been nice for him after all the injury troubles that followed his first two caps in 2012.

At 33 years of age, Miyaichi is still playing with Yokohoma F. Marinos, and he won the JLeague with them back in 2022, adding to the two FA Cups he technically won between Wigan in 2013 and Arsenal in 2014.

Speaking back in June on why he wants to keep playing, Miyaichi said: "I think having fun is the most important thing. It's what fuels your growth. I still have the desire to keep improving and still feel the same way I always did. Showing people something that makes them think is cool on the pitch is what I'm there for."

Miyaichi's never-ending battles with injury must have made for a tough time, but fueled by his love for the game, he never gave up, and perseverance has allowed him to keep playing at the age of 33. Things could have gone a very different way for the winger, even if his biggest hopes were not fulfilled.

Tzolis' 64% pass rate and 16 turnovers underlined a stale forward line, forcing Arteta into a risky selection call.

NBA announces $11 billion investment plan to develop, modernize arenas for NBA Europe

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Is European basketball ready for modern American-style NBA arenas? With adjacent entertainment districts around them?

The NBA announced plans Wednesday for a €10 billion (nearly $11 million at current exchange rates) initiative to pay for "the development and renovation of basketball arenas – and the sports and entertainment districts around them – across Europe." To make this happen, the NBA assembled a group of heavy-hitter global investment firms including Apollo Sports Capital; Ares Management; Arctos, a business of KKR; Blackstone; HPS Investment Partners, a part of BlackRock; J.P. Morgan; Sixth Street and Stonepeak, among others.

"We've seen firsthand how state-of-the-art arenas and the related developments around them can transform basketball teams and their surrounding communities," NBA Commissioner Adam Silver said in a statement announcing the initiative. "As we prepare to launch a new league in Europe alongside FIBA, we see a significant opportunity to help clubs develop facilities which both build on the unique traditions that define European basketball while also elevating the fan experience and attracting many more live events."

This is about arenas for NBA Europe teams, the league the NBA and partner FIBA plan to at least soft launch next October. That said, this initiative is entirely separate from the team investment process.

League officials have been open from the start that they see opportunity in Europe, not just because of the fan base, but because the arena infrastructure there does not maximize profitability — the NBA believes European clubs are leaving money on the table. Part of the plan to make NBA Europe profitable is to change that.

In its announcement, the NBA put it this way: "The investors will look to partner with participating clubs, ownership groups, local developers and other service providers to develop world-class arenas that improve the fan experience and, where appropriate, mixed-use districts around them. Depending on local needs and opportunities, these projects could include housing, workplaces, restaurants, retail and public spaces, helping to revitalize communities where people live, work and play. "

The NBA has long championed the idea that new world-class arenas drive economic growth in the cities where they are placed, not only through ticket sales and jobs created, but also by revitalizing parts of a city if there are housing and entertainment districts (with restaurants, theaters and more) around them. However, multiple studies suggest this is more a redistribution of wealth and spending from other parts of a city to the new district.

Europe does not have many arenas like the ones NBA teams often play in. The target cities for NBA Europe include Paris, Rome, London, Manchester, Lyon, Madrid, Barcelona, Athens, Berlin and more. However, those cities have not been finalized as the bidding process continues.

When the airliner is at or below 15,500 feet, its system can issue a traffic advisory for another airplane whose transponder replies without giving its altitude. The cockpit display leaves off the data tag. It seems at least as plausible (probably more so) than a UFO. So is a false alert.

That said, there were several flase collision warnings approaching Washington National last year, and they were all with an aircraft at exactly 2,300 feet – just like the controller reporting an aircraft at 2,300 feet here.

And American Airlines pilots have reported seeing things out the window. Over Arizona in 2018, a Learjet pilot reported something passing overhead. A controller asked American Airlines flight 1095 to keep an eye out. That crew saw something reflecting light several thousand feet above them, too.

In 2021, a pilot on American Airlines flight 2292 from Cincinnati to Phoenix described a fast-moving cylinder going right over the top of the jet in New Mexico. American initially told me they couldn’t tie the call to their crew. After a debrief, they confirmed it. The FBI closed its file because it lacked jurisdiction.

Listen to American Airlines flight 2292 over New Mexico in 2021

The day before the Baltimore exchange, the Pentagon released 40 more UFO documents and three videos. One 1952 CIA briefing revisits sightings around the airport this flight had just left. That July, Washington National and Andrews both picked up unexplained radar returns. The Air Force sent up interceptors but came back empty.

My favorite exhibit from Friday is a single radar slide from a 2023 case. Civilian radar got eight hits in 84 seconds. If one object made all eight, it averaged at least 1,650 miles per hour.

Five days before Thanksgiving, the American Eagle pilot already knew what he was going to do with his controller’s report. “It’s a good story to tell my family. I like that.”

Camino Day 21: Hórreos and the Father of the Modern Camino

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

We leave so early from Casa Cruceiro de Ferreiros that we have to unlock the door and let ourselves out. I can tell Suzanne is itching to go, but we only have one headlamp, which effectively yokes us until dawn, or first coffee. Then she will rabbit away. The last few days we’ve not walked together, which is not to say we’re walking alone—there are 1,000s of pilgrims to walk with.

The Way isn’t that different from days past, and the *turigrinos* are getting harder to distinguish from the rest of us. Yes, they have their cute little daypacks, but the clothes aren’t shiny anymore, and I see a weariness, a gift of several days on trail.

We walk by stone fences on rolling, rural lanes, and I try to imagine what it must have been like when resources were so slim that split stone was the material available for fences. There is a gentle roll to The Way, and we pass the 100.000k marker to Santiago—unremarkably just there, with no fanfare, like 1,000s of others. The end is 62 miles away!

Time and millions of pilgrims have worn down the Camino

I am so fascinated by the hórreos of Galicia

We walk between six-foot high dry lay stone fences, and later on a sunken path, worn down by this pilgrimage. I admire hórreos of every make: stone, brick, wooden, and a mix. They seem randomly dropped more than thoughtfully placed, at odd angles with what is around them. I know that many have been here since the 13th century, but neither they nor the landscape yield to one another. Hórreos evolved to dry millet until the arrival of the New World crop: corn. Galicia’s climatic peculiarities prevent grain from completely drying in the field. Done mechanically today, in years past the ears were shucked and stored whole to allow air to pass through, to cool and dry the grain to prevent spoilage and fermentation.

In an information sheet I found at tonight’s hostel, it reads:

In 1973, a Decree was approved by the Spanish state to protect all the old granaries and huts existing in Galicia and Asturias, which attempted to address the consequences of the abandonment of the rural way of life and the loss of use of the granaries.

I guess I won’t be transporting any hórreos home to my backyard.

We’re in pulpo territory, and find pulperías and copper cauldrons smoking away outside roadside bars. The Galician preparation for octopus is smoked paprika, sea salt, and a splash of olive oil, all served on a seven-inch wooden plate and eaten with toothpicks. Suzanne largely doesn’t partake because the octopus is known to be an intelligent sea creature; I’d feel bad too if they weren’t so tasty. Kinda like bacon—if they are so smart, then why so tasty? It is a tragic, delicious bargain we’ve made with nature’s smartest species. On a lighter note, we’ve stumbled into a soft homemade cheese, queso con miel, that is drizzled with local Galician honey, and the seasonal padrón peppers.

Padrón peppers – fried with olive oil and sprinkled with course salt.

I walk with David and his mother Maxine, from Australia. She is a 12-month cancer survivor, and as David tells the story, when she was at her lowest… she interjects “so exhausted I couldn’t even hold a cell phone” David pitched walking the Camino. That was 18 months ago, and here they are. I think back to other Aussies I’ve met, and each seemed to be recovering from a major health crisis.

Then, there was Fritz, born in Nebraska, but sent away to boarding school at a young age and never returned, living abroad and traveling the world in the business of finance. He tells me a different side of the story of how the modern Camino came to be. His friend was named Manuel Fraga Iribarne, who in the mid-1990s was the regional governor of Galicia, then one of the poorest, most isolated agricultural regions of Spain. At that time, the Camino was a quiet trail mostly walked by devout Catholics, historians, or avid hikers. (When they invent time travel, this is where I’m returning, to walk the Camino of those days). Working with Father Elías (the priest of O Cebreiro), who was the spiritual father and painted the yellow arrows to guide the way, Fraga had the political and marketing know-how to rebuild the infrastructure we enjoy today. One hears of Father Elías in Catholic stories, but not Manuel Fraga Iribarne. Thank you, Fritz.

This is his eighth Camino, and he is reluctantly walking it because it is a graduation requirement for the two-week course he took in Santiago. Fritz has been so interesting to listen to and walk with, but I recognize in him that, like myself, much of our success has been given to us through the privilege of the stations our parents gave us. As men, much of how we see ourselves is through our work, and Fritz, David, and the other unhappy conservative I walked with are all now retired. Their importance and reason for being is gone, and I think they are looking for something that replaces what they have lost. SH*T, I think to myself, this is the third man I’ve walked with that turns out to be angry and older—what is she, The Camino, trying to teach me?

He has seen the world and chooses to be angry. I see his unwavering support of the current administration being driven more out of retribution for the direction the country was going than any hope or vision for its future. If our country was a garden and overrun by weeds, his solution would be to drown it in Roundup and pave it over. No more weeds. My Dad would say that weeds are just misplaced plants and can have a lot to say diagnostically about the health of the soil. The goal isn’t to keep a weed-free garden, but to produce food, knowing that for the health of the soil, weeds must coexist.

So I walk with Fritz until I can’t. Yes, he is a fast walker, unencumbered, and fresh on the Camino, but I tire of his raw anger, in such contrast to the Anglican faith he professes. My exit comes when he sees his pickup van in case he is ready to stop walking. He goes over to check in and I, who have no pickup van, sit and rest. Fritz continues on, walking over the hill and out of my Camino never to be seen by me again.

Venture and Ms.Venture finished the Camino de Santiago via the Portuguese Way summer of '24. And Feb 2025, Japan's Kumano Kodō, and later that Sunday the first third of the Oregon Coast Trail. I'm 3026 we walked The French Way from Burgos. We are a couple of 60+ Texpats living in Colorado slowly walking as we contemplate what our next season of life might look like. Venture's interest in thru-hiking came while they were in lockdown in Hanoi, Vietnam during the pandemic. He read AWOL on the Appalachian Trail and was never the same. He has walked The French Camino, 10% of the AT, The Lone Star Trail, and 50% of The Colorado Trail. Thanks for joining the adventure.

GTA 6: How To Permanently Increase All Stats

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

There are three main stats you'll need to pay attention to throughout your Grand Theft Auto 6 playthrough: Strength, Cardio, and Focus. Jason and Lucia each have their own individual stats, which play an important role in everything from surviving intense shootouts to escaping the police and clearing heat on foot.

Strength determines your maximum health, Cardio governs your stamina, and Focus dictates how long you can slow down time during combat. Improving all three will make for a much smoother playthrough, especially as missions become more challenging later on. While certain activities provide temporary benefits, there are also ways to permanently increase each of these stats in GTA 6, making it well worth investing time in both characters outside the main story.

You can check each character's current stats by opening their individual profiles in the Jason & Lucia menu, while the Fitness app on your phone allows you to track your workout progress. Each of the three main stats can be improved through different activities, though neglecting your Strength and Cardio workout windows will eventually cause you to lose some of your progress.

However, certain side missions and Easter eggs in GTA 6 reward you with permanent increases to your maximum health, stamina, or Focus. Unlike the gains you make at the gym, these bonuses don't require regular maintenance to retain. Most of these encounters aren't marked on your map, and there's almost no indication that completing them will improve your stats. The following are all the currently reported encounters that offer permanent stat increases:

One of the more bizarre ways to permanently increase your maximum health in GTA 6 involves participating in a satanic ritual. Head to the northern area of Port Gellhorn between midnight and 4 AM, where you'll find two metalheads hanging out inside an open garage illuminated by red lights.

Approach them while you're alone and agree to accompany them in their van. You'll eventually arrive at a campfire, which you'll need to stare into to trigger a sequence that transports you to an unholy realm. Make your way through the encounter and defeat Satan to receive a permanent increase to your maximum health. You'll also be rewarded with the unique Hellspawn Comshot 12 shotgun, a new tattoo, and a Circles of Hell shirt.

There are several different ways to permanently increase your maximum stamina in GTA 6, making Cardio one of the easiest stats to improve without having to constantly worry about getting to the gym.

To initiate "The Final Flush," head to the men's public restroom on Corsair Key and look for the Stop! Don't Flush poster. Scan the QR code using the WAiNK It app on your phone and respond to the subsequent message with "TRUTH."

You'll eventually arrange a nighttime meeting with Dr. Merle at Frank's Waffles in Tequesta, after which you'll need to accompany him to a sewage treatment facility. Once you've completed Merle's rather unfortunate experiment, make sure you enter the chamber yourself. Doing so will reward you with a permanent increase to your maximum stamina.

Starting in Chapter 2, you'll be able to find a "Pilot Wanted" flyer outside the Starlet Motel office, which introduces you to Kyle McKittrick and his farming operation. Pursue Kyle's questline until you've completed the "Collect Chemicals for Kyle" mission.

Kyle will eventually offer you the opportunity to sample one of his enormous, chemically enhanced strawberries. Make sure you accept his offer, as eating the strawberry will permanently increase your maximum stamina. You'll be able to return to the farm and eat additional strawberries for temporary benefits, but you won't receive another permanent stat increase from doing so.

Head to the construction site along the eastern shore of Lake Tallaloko, just north of Millport, where you'll find an open orange shipping container. Inside the container is a mysterious object labeled "???" that you can interact with to consume a mushroom. Doing so will trigger an elaborate hallucination involving a surreal platforming sequence. You'll need to navigate a series of obstacles and floating platforms to reach the end of the course.

Successfully completing the obstacle course will permanently increase your maximum stamina, and you'll also unlock the Mind Maze Left Leg Award tattoo.

While exploring the wooded areas east of Port Gellhorn, you may come across a group of men participating in a shooting competition. They'll initially invite you to demonstrate your shooting skills by hitting various targets before offering you a $250 wager on an iguana hunting competition.

Accept the wager and shoot more iguanas than your opponent before the timer expires. Winning the competition has been reported to reward players with a permanent increase to their maximum Focus, in addition to the cash prize.

One of the more elaborate mysteries in GTA 6 involves investigating the assassination of President Joe Russo. Following the various clues will eventually lead you to a secret underground government facility in northern Mariana County.

Inside the facility, you'll be able to unlock a room containing a television and a chair. Sitting in the chair triggers a strange sequence set inside a hospital, where you'll need to solve a series of puzzles involving manipulating time.

Complete the sequence and its final confrontation with President Russo to receive a permanent increase to your maximum Focus, as well as a new tattoo. Make sure to collect the unique Brainwasher 1922 pistol near the television when you return to the bunker.

You can also substantially improve your regular Focus stat by participating in shooting range challenges. One of the quickest ways to do this is to visit Rodgers Ranch & Shooting Range near Port Gellhorn, purchase the $5 waiver, and repeatedly complete the Market Range Beginner challenge.

Other recreational activities, such as fishing, playing pool, and golfing, will also contribute toward your Focus progression, though these aren't the same as the one-time maximum Focus upgrades awarded through hidden encounters.

Arguably the most worthwhile side quest you can pursue in GTA 6 involves investigating the UFO encounter near the Starlet Motel. Starting in Chapter 2, head to the wooded area east of Port Gellhorn at around 2 AM and look for an abandoned vehicle with its lights flashing.

Follow the trail leading away from the vehicle, where you'll encounter a woman being abducted by a UFO. Completing this initial encounter will eventually result in your own abduction, after which you'll need to sleep at a safehouse until you experience a strange dream aboard an alien spacecraft. Successfully completing the entire questline will reward you with permanent increases to your maximum health, stamina, and Focus, making it one of the most rewarding optional encounters to complete early in your GTA 6 playthrough.

You can check out all our GTA 6 guides here.
